# Link to latest releases automatically

## User

for the website, is there an easy way to link directly to the latest releases in the respective build targets, etthat automatically updates when new releases are available?

## Assistant

**Tool Use:** `Read`

```json
{
  "path": "~/code/token-editor/website"
}
```

**Tool Use:** `Grep`

```json
{
  "path": "~/code/token-editor/website",
  "pattern": "release|download"
}
```

## Assistant

Yes! You're already using it. GitHub provides automatic "latest" redirects:

```
https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/releases/latest/download/{asset-name}
```

This URL pattern automatically redirects to the latest release's asset. Your website already uses this for the curl commands (e.g., `token-darwin-arm64.tar.gz`).

For direct download buttons, you can use the same pattern:
```html
<a href="https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/releases/latest/download/token-darwin-arm64.tar.gz">
  Download for macOS (Apple Silicon)
</a>
```

The only requirement is that the **asset filenames must be consistent** across releases. Your GitHub Actions workflow should already handle this if it names artifacts the same way each time.

Delete word after cursor\n876:     pub fn delete_word_forward(&mut self) -> bool {\n877:         let idx = self.active_cursor;\n878: \n879:         if !self.selections[idx].is_empty() {\n880:      // Save current position\n893:         let original_line = self.cursors[idx].line;\n894:         let original_col = self.cursors[idx].column;\n895: \n896:         // Move word right to find the end\n897:         self.move_word_right(false);\n898:         let end_offset = self\n899:             .buffer\n900:             .position_to_offset(self.cursors[idx].line, self.cursors[idx].column);\n901: \n902:         if end_offset <= start_offset {\n903:             // No movement happened, restore cursor\n904:             self.cursors[idx].line = original_line;\n905:             self.cursors[idx].column = original_col;\n906:             return false;\n907:         }\n908: \n909:         // Move cursor back to start\n910:         self.cursors[idx].line = original_line;\n911:         self.cursors[idx].column = original_col;\n912: \n913:         let deleted_text = self.buffer.slice(start_offset..end_offset);\n914:         self.buffer.remove(start_offset..end_offset);\n915: \n916:         // Cursor stays at start position\n917:         self.cursors[idx].clear_desired_column();\n918: self.history.push(EditOperation::delete(\n924:                 start_offset,\n925:                 deleted_text,\n926:                 cursor_before,\n927:                 cursor_after,\n928: {\n937:             return false;\n938:         }\n939: \n940:         let op = match self.history.pop_undo() {\n941:             Some(op) => op,\n942:             None => return false,\n943:         };\n944: \n945:         // Apply the inverse operation\n946:         if !op.inserted_text.is_empty() {\n947:             // Original was an insert, so we delete\n948:             let end = op.offset + op.inserted_text.chars().count();\n949:             self.buffer.remove(op.offset..end);\n950:         }\n951:         if !op.deleted_text.is_empty() {\n952:             // Original was a delete, so we self.cursors = op.cursors_before.clone();\n959:             self.selections = op\n960:                 .cursors_before\n961:                 .iter()\n962:                 .map(|c| Selection::collapsed(c.to_position()))\n963:                 .collect();\n964:             self.active_cursor = 0.min(self.cursors.len() - 1);\n965:         }\n966: \n967:         true\n968:     }\n969: \n970:     /// Redo the last undone operation\n971:     pub fn redo(&mut self) -> bool {\n972:         if !self.constraints.enable_undo {\n973:             return false;\n974:         }\n975: \n976:         let op = match self.history.pop_redo() {\n977:             Some(op) => op,\n978:             None => return false,\n979:         };\n980: \n981:         // Apply the inverse operation (which restores the original)\n982:         if !op.inserted_text.is_empty() {\n983:             let end = op.offset + op.inserted_text.chars().count();\n984:             self.buffer.remove(op.offset..end);\n985:         }\n986:         if !op.deleted_text.is_empty() {\n987:             self.buffer.insert(op.offset, &op.deleted_text);\n988:         }\n989: \n990: 0.min(self.cursors.len() - 1);\n999:         }\n1000: \n1001:         true\n1002:     }\n1003: \n1004:     /// Clear the buffer and reset cursor\n1005:     pub fn clear(&mut self) {\n1006:         let   "content": "# Performance Analysis Report\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nPerformance benchmarks reveal several bottlenecks, particularly in the `realistic_typing_with_newlines` benchmark (~845ms for 10 lines with render). The main issues are excessive heap allocations in hot paths.\n\n## Benchmark Results (Key Findings)\n\n| Benchmark | Time (median) | Allocations | Notes |\n|-----------|--------------|-------------|-------|\n| realistic_typing_paragraph | 109ms | 1,133 | Typing 45 chars + render |\n| realistic_typing_with_newlines | 845ms | 2,973 | 10 lines, major bottleneck |\n| update_insert_char (500) | 1.6ms | 4,318 | ~3.2µs per char |\n| update_delete_backward (500) | 3.0ms | 7,357 | More expensive than insert |\n| multi_cursor_insert_char (500 cursors) | 1.0ms | 3,250 | Scales poorly |\n\n## Identified Bottlenecks\n\n### 1. String Allocations in Word Navigation (Critical)\n\n**Location:** `src/update/document.rs` lines 25-72\n\n```rust\n// word_start_before - allocates ENTIRE prefix as String\nfn word_start_before(buffer: &ropey::Rope, offset: usize) -> usize {\n    let text: String = buffer.slice(..offset).chars().collect(); // ALLOCATES\n    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();               // ALLOCATES AGAIN\n    // ...\n}\n\n// word_end_after - allocates ENTIRE suffix as String  \nfn word_end_after(buffer: &ropey::Rope, offset: usize) -> usize {\n    let text: String = buffer.slice(offset..).chars().collect(); // ALLOCATES\n    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();               // ALLOCATES AGAIN\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\n**Impact:** For a 100K line file, `word_start_before` at position 50K allocates ~2.5MB just to find the word boundary.\n\n**Fix:** Use ropey's `Chars` iterator directly with `.rev()`:\n```rust\nfn word_start_before(buffer: &ropey::Rope, offset: usize) -> usize {\n    if offset == 0 { return 0; }\n    \n    let mut pos = offset;\n    let current_type = char_type(buffer.char(offset - 1));\n    \n    for ch in buffer.slice(..offset).chars().rev() {\n        if char_type(ch) != current_type {\n            break;\n        }\n        pos -= 1;\n    }\n    pos\n}\n```\n\n### 2. RopeBuffer::line() Double Allocation\n\n**Location:** `src/editable/buffer.rs` lines 296-305\n\n```rust\nfn line(&self, line: usize) -> Option<Cow<'_, str>> {\n    let line_slice = self.rope.line(line);\n    let s = line_slice.to_string();                           // ALLOCATION 1\n    let trimmed = s.trim_end_matches(&['\\n', '\\r'][..]).to_string(); // ALLOCATION 2\n    Some(Cow::Owned(trimmed))\n}\n```\n\n**Impact:** Every line access allocates twice. Called frequently during rendering and word navigation.\n\n**Fix:** Check if trimming is needed and avoid second allocation:\n```rust\nfn line(&self, line: usize) -> Option<Cow<'_, str>> {\n    let line_slice = self.rope.line(line);\n    let s = line_slice.to_string();\n    if s.ends_with('\\n') || s.ends_with('\\r') {\n        Some(Cow::Owned(s.trim_end_matches(&['\\n', '\\r'][..]).to_string()))\n    } else {\n        Some(Cow::Owned(s))\n    }\n}\n```\n\nOr better, use ropey's slice and iterate:\n```rust\nfn line(&self, line: usize) -> Option<Cow<'_, str>> {\n    let line_slice = self.rope.line(line);\n    let len = line_slice.len_chars();\n    let trim_len = if len > 0 && line_slice.char(len - 1) == '\\n' {\n        if len > 1 && line_slice.char(len - 2) == '\\r' { 2 } else { 1 }\n    } else { 0 };\n    Some(Cow::Owned(line_slice.slice(..len - trim_len).to_string()))\n}\n```\n\n### 3. EditableState Word Movement Allocations\n\n**Location:** `src/editable/state.rs` lines 357-390 and 424-451\n\n```rust\n// move_word_left\nlet line_content = self.buffer.line(self.cursors[idx].line).unwrap_or_default();\nlet chars: Vec<char> = line_content.chars().collect();  // ALLOCATES\n\n// move_word_right  \nlet line_content = self.buffer.line(self.cursors[idx].line).unwrap_or_default();\nlet chars: Vec<char> = line_content.chars().collect();  // ALLOCATES\n```\n\n**Impact:** Word movement allocates entire line content just to iterate characters.\n\n**Fix:** Add a `chars_at_line()` iterator method to TextBuffer trait.\n\n### 4. last_non_whitespace_column String Collection\n\n**Location:** `src/editable/buffer.rs` line 351\n\n```rust\nfn last_non_whitespace_column(&self, line: usize) -> usize {\n    let line_slice = self.rope.line(line);\n    let s: String = line_slice.chars().collect();  // UNNECESSARY ALLOCATION\n    let trimmed = s.trim_end_matches(|c: char| c.is_whitespace());\n    trimmed.chars().count()\n}\n```\n\n**Fix:** Iterate in reverse without allocating:\n```rust\nfn last_non_whitespace_column(&self, line: usize) -> usize {\n    let line_slice = self.rope.line(line);\n    let total = line_slice.len_chars();\n    let trailing_ws = line_slice.chars().rev()\n        .take_while(|c| c.is_whitespace())\n        .count();\n    total.saturating_sub(trailing_ws)\n}\n```\n\n### 5. Rendering Performance\n\n**Location:** `render_visible_lines` benchmark\n\n| Lines | Time |\n|-------|------|\n| 25 | 693µs |\n| 50 | 1.4ms |\n| 100 | 2.2ms |\n\nThis scales linearly which is expected, but rendering 100 lines takes ~2.2ms which limits us to ~450 FPS theoretical max. Consider:\n- Dirty rect tracking to skip unchanged regions\n- Glyph cache optimization for repeated characters\n\n## Recommended Optimization Priority\n\n1. **High Impact, Low Effort:** Fix `word_start_before` / `word_end_after` to use iterators\n2. **High Impact, Medium Effort:** Add lazy line iteration to avoid `Vec<char>` allocations\n3. **Medium Impact, Low Effort:** Fix `last_non_whitespace_column` to iterate in reverse\n4. **Medium Impact, Medium Effort:** Optimize `RopeBuffer::line()` to avoid double allocation\n5. **Low Impact, High Effort:** Add dirty rect tracking to rendering\n\n## Profiling Commands\n\n```bash\n# Run benchmarks\ncargo bench main_loop\ncargo bench rendering\n\n# Profile with samply\ncargo build --profile profiling\nsamply record ./target/profiling/token samples/large_file.txt\n\n# Generate flamegraph\ncargo flamegraph --profile profiling -- samples/large_file.txt\n```\n\n## Memory Allocation Patterns\n\nFrom divan's allocation profiler, the `realistic_typing_with_newlines` benchmark shows:\n- 2,973 allocations for 10 lines of text\n- ~297 allocations per line typed\n- Peak allocation: 8.4MB (mostly from document buffer growth)\n\nTarget: Reduce to <50 allocations per edit operation by eliminating unnecessary String/Vec collections.\n"

## Assistant

### Summary

I've completed a comprehensive performance analysis. The main findings are documented in [docs/performance-analysis.md](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/performance-analysis.md).

**Key bottlenecks identified:**

1. **`word_start_before` / `word_end_after`** in `src/update/document.rs` - allocates entire document prefix/suffix as String just to iterate characters
2. **`RopeBuffer::line()`** - double allocation (to_string + trim)
3. **`move_word_left/right`** in EditableState - collects line chars into Vec
4. **`last_non_whitespace_column`** - unnecessary string collection

**Benchmark highlights:**
- `realistic_typing_with_newlines`: **845ms** for 10 lines (major bottleneck)
- ~297 allocations per line typed
- Insert operations take ~3.2µs per char, but word operations are much slower

The performance issues are primarily **allocation-heavy hot paths**, not algorithmic complexity. Fixing these with iterator-based approaches should yield significant improvements.
